Um... well, I wouldn't really say I set out to lose a bunch of weight on purpose, if that makes sense? I hope it doesn't seem like I'm doing this just to worry people or be difficult. It's really more about efficiency, I think.

Um... unacceptable? Oh, I don't know if I'd use a word quite that harsh. I hope I don't come across as someone who's just completely obsessed with their appearance, especially at my age.

Um... well, no, I don't really think so. I hope that doesn't sound stubborn of me to say. I know I'm on the slender side, but I just feel like I'm maintaining a good, active weight for my work.

I suppose \"worried\" is a strong word, but... yes, I guess I do get a little anxious about it. I hope you don't think I'm being vain or silly for my age. It's just... \n\nIf I eat anything heavy, or, you know, if I let myself have the richer foods we used to make for the holidays... I start fretting right away. I just get this awful feeling that I'll gain weight and... and just lose this grip I've worked so very hard to keep on myself and my routine. It's just easier to avoid those things so I don't have to feel that way. Does that... I mean, is it normal to want to just keep things under control like that?\n\nDoctor: Do you think about your weight throughout the day?\n\nPatient: Um... well, I wouldn't say I think about it *all* the time. I hope that's the right answer. I mean, I have my carpentry work to focus on, and that keeps me very busy. \n\nBut I suppose... yes, it does cross my mind, maybe more than it should? Just little things, like checking if my work apron still fits the same way, or making sure I haven't gotten... softer, I guess. It's just a way to make sure I'm staying on track and not letting myself go. I don't want to be a bother to anyone by getting out of shape. Do you think it's okay that I just like to keep an eye on things like that?\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like you would lose control if you started eating more or heavier foods?\n\nPatient: Um... well, yes, I suppose that's exactly it. I hope it doesn't sound too... extreme, when I put it like that? \n\nLike I was saying before, if I were to eat anything heavy, or... or let myself have those richer holiday foods... I just start worrying right away. It's like this instant fear that I'll gain weight and just... completely lose the grip I've worked so incredibly hard to keep on myself. It feels like if I let go even a little bit with heavier foods, everything else will just unravel. I just want to make sure I'm doing the right thing by staying disciplined. That makes sense, doesn't it? To want to keep that sense of control?\n\nDoctor: Do you think you would look unacceptable to yourself if you gained weight?\n\nPatient: Um... unacceptable? Oh, I don't know if I'd use a word quite that harsh. I hope I don't come across as someone who's just completely obsessed with their appearance, especially at my age. \n\nI just... I suppose I'd be disappointed in myself, more than anything. Like I wasn't taking proper care of myself anymore. I've always been someone who takes pride in being neat and put-together, and... well, gaining weight might just make me feel a bit untidy, I guess? Not unacceptable, just... maybe not my best self. I just want to make sure I'm presenting myself well. Is that... do you think that's a normal way to feel about it?\n\nDoctor: Do you think your weight is currently lower than a healthy weight for someone of your age and height?\n\nPatient: Um... well, no, I don't really think so. I hope that doesn't sound stubborn of me to say. I know I'm on the slender side, but I just feel like I'm maintaining a good, active weight for my work. \n\nI mean, my husband and... and the apprentices at the shop, they keep saying I look too thin lately. They worry, which is sweet of them, I suppose. But I always just tell them I'm perfectly fine. I think sometimes people just... well, they confuse being disciplined and taking care of yourself with being sick. Especially when everyone else seems to be getting a bit heavier as they get older. I'm just trying to stay fit and capable, you know? Do you think it's okay that I just want to stay at a weight where I feel productive?\n\nDoctor: Do you think you look too thin now?\n\nPatient: Um... well, like I was just mentioning a moment ago, I really don't see it that way myself. I hope I don't sound like a broken record, repeating myself so soon! \n\nIt's just... I know my husband and the young apprentices at the shop keep telling me I look too thin right now. They bring it up a lot, actually. But I always reassure them that I'm completely fine. When I look at myself, I just see someone who is lean and ready to work. Like I said before, I really believe people nowadays just confuse having a bit of self-discipline with being sick. I'm just taking care of my body so I can keep up with the physical demands of my carpentry. That's a positive way to look at it, isn't it? Um... well, I wouldn't say it's entirely about my appearance. I hope I'm explaining this right, I just... I don't want to sound vain.

Um... well, I wouldn't say it's ruined anything, exactly. I hope that doesn't sound like I'm ignoring their concerns. I know they care. 

It's just... well, I still go to the shop every day, of course. I wouldn't miss it. Um... well, no, not really. I mean, I hope I'm answering this right, but I don't think I avoid things.

I suppose I'd be disappointed in myself, more than anything. Like I wasn't taking proper care of myself anymore. I've always been someone who takes pride in being neat and put-together, and... well, gaining weight might just make me feel a bit untidy, I guess? Not unacceptable, just... maybe not my best self. I just want to make sure I'm presenting myself well. I hope that doesn't sound stubborn of me to say. I know I'm on the slender side, but I just feel like I'm maintaining a good, active weight for my work. \n\nI mean, my husband and... and the apprentices at the shop, they keep saying I look too thin lately. They worry, which is sweet of them, I suppose. But I always just tell them I'm perfectly fine. I think sometimes people just... well, they confuse being disciplined and taking care of yourself with being sick. Especially when everyone else seems to be getting a bit heavier as they get older. I'm just trying to stay fit and capable, you know? Do you think it's okay that I just want to stay at a weight where I feel productive?\n\nDoctor: Do you think you look too thin now?\n\nPatient: Um... well, like I was just mentioning a moment ago, I really don't see it that way myself. I hope I don't sound like a broken record, repeating myself so soon! \n\nIt's just... I know my husband and the young apprentices at the shop keep telling me I look too thin right now. They bring it up a lot, actually. But I always reassure them that I'm completely fine. When I look at myself, I just see someone who is lean and ready to work. Like I said before, I really believe people nowadays just confuse having a bit of self-discipline with being sick. I'm just taking care of my body so I can keep up with the physical demands of my carpentry. That's a positive way to look at it, isn't it? I just want to make sure I'm doing the right thing for my health, rather than just letting myself go.\n\nDoctor: Do you think your fear of gaining weight is related to how you want to look?\n\nPatient: Um... well, I wouldn't say it's entirely about my appearance. I hope I'm explaining this right, I just... I don't want to sound vain. \n\nIt's really more about... oh, how do I put it... just feeling capable and in control, I suppose? I worry that I'll gain weight and just... lose the grip I've worked so incredibly hard to keep. \n\nI really don't want you to think I'm being silly or overly dramatic about it. It's just that it takes a lot of effort to stay disciplined, and I don't want everything to just slip away from me. It's a good thing to want to hold onto your structure and your good habits, isn't it? Do you think that's a reasonable way to look at it?\n\nDoctor: Has your fear of gaining weight affected your relationships with family or friends?\n\nPatient: Um... well, I wouldn't say it's ruined anything, exactly. But lately, my husband and the apprentices... they've sort of started taking over more of the heavier lifting and the harder work. And they're always checking on me, asking if I need to sit down or take a break. It's very kind of them, it really is, but... oh, I don't know, it just makes the whole place feel a little less like my own, if that makes sense? ========== OUTPUT [patient] ==========
Um... well, no, I certainly wouldn't say it's the *only* thing. I mean, I hope I don't sound superficial, but... there are so many other things that make a person, well, presentable and nice to be around. 

Like being kind, or... or keeping a tidy home, and working hard at your trade. I think those things matter a great deal, don't you? But... oh, I suppose I do feel like my weight is... well, it's the one thing I can really keep a firm hand on. If I can just stay small and neat, then... then maybe the rest of me looks a bit more put-together, too? 

I just want to look respectable, you know? Like someone who takes care of herself and doesn't let things fall to pieces. It's perfectly normal to want to present your best self to the world, isn't it? I hope you don't think I'm too focused on it. I just try to do my best.

I didn't want to upset anyone or be difficult, so I agreed to come in. \n\nBut honestly, I feel like I'm doing okay? Being this light... it actually really helps me keep up with my carpentry shop. I have to move around a lot and be on my feet, and this way I just don't get slowed down. Does that... I hope it doesn't seem like I'm doing this just to worry people or be difficult. It's really more about efficiency, I think. \n\nLike, on my workshop days, I usually just have tea... and maybe just a few careful bites of something small. Stopping for a real, sit-down meal just feels so unnecessary when I'm in the middle of a project, and honestly, it kind of throws off the control I’ve got over my schedule and my day. It’s just so much easier to keep things streamlined and light. Is that... I mean, does that sound reasonable to you, for someone who works on their feet all day?\n\nDoctor: Are you worried about gaining weight?\n\nPatient: Well, um... I suppose \"worried\" is a strong word, but... yes, I guess I do get a little anxious about it. I hope you don't think I'm being vain or silly for my age. It's just... \n\nIf I eat anything heavy, or, you know, if I let myself have the richer foods we used to make for the holidays... I start fretting right away. I just get this awful feeling that I'll gain weight and... and just lose this grip I've worked so very hard to keep on myself and my routine. It's just easier to avoid those things so I don't have to feel that way. Does that... I mean, is it normal to want to just keep things under control like that?\n\nDoctor: Do you think about your weight throughout the day?\n\nPatient: Um... well, I wouldn't say I think about it *all* the time. I hope that's the right answer. I mean, I have my carpentry work to focus on, and that keeps me very busy. \n\nBut I suppose... yes, it does cross my mind, maybe more than it should? Just little things, like checking if my work apron still fits the same way, or making sure I haven't gotten... softer, I guess. It's just a way to make sure I'm staying on track and not letting myself go. I don't want to be a bother to anyone by getting out of shape. Do you think it's okay that I just like to keep an eye on things like that?\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like you would lose control if you started eating more or heavier foods?\n\nPatient: Um... well, yes, I suppose that's exactly it. I hope it doesn't sound too... extreme, when I put it like that? \n\nLike I was saying before, if I were to eat anything heavy, or... or let myself have those richer holiday foods... I just start worrying right away. It's like this instant fear that I'll gain weight and just... completely lose the grip I've worked so incredibly hard to keep on myself. It feels like if I let go even a little bit with heavier foods, everything else will just unravel. I just want to make sure I'm doing the right thing by staying disciplined. That makes sense, doesn't it? To want to keep that sense of control?\n\nDoctor: Do you think you would look unacceptable to yourself if you gained weight?\n\nPatient: Um... unacceptable? Oh, I don't know if I'd use a word quite that harsh. I hope I don't come across as someone who's just completely obsessed with their appearance, especially at my age. \n\nI just... I suppose I'd be disappointed in myself, more than anything. Like I wasn't taking proper care of myself anymore. I've always been someone who takes pride in being neat and put-together, and... well, gaining weight might just make me feel a bit untidy, I guess? Not unacceptable, just... maybe not my best self. I just want to make sure I'm presenting myself well. Is that... do you think that's a normal way to feel about it?\n\nDoctor: Do you think your weight is currently lower than a healthy weight for someone of your age and height?\n\nPatient: Um... well, no, I don't really think so. I hope that doesn't sound stubborn of me to say. I know I'm on the slender side, but I just feel like I'm maintaining a good, active weight for my work. \n\nI mean, my husband and... and the apprentices at the shop, they keep saying I look too thin lately. They worry, which is sweet of them, I suppose. But I always just tell them I'm perfectly fine. I think sometimes people just... well, they confuse being disciplined and taking care of yourself with being sick. Especially when everyone else seems to be getting a bit heavier as they get older. I'm just trying to stay fit and capable, you know? Do you think it's okay that I just want to stay at a weight where I feel productive?\n\nDoctor: Do you think you look too thin now?\n\nPatient: Um... well, like I was just mentioning a moment ago, I really don't see it that way myself. I hope I don't sound like a broken record, repeating myself so soon! \n\nIt's just... I know my husband and the young apprentices at the shop keep telling me I look too thin right now. They bring it up a lot, actually. But I always reassure them that I'm completely fine. When I look at myself, I just see someone who is lean and ready to work. Like I said before, I really believe people nowadays just confuse having a bit of self-discipline with being sick. I'm just taking care of my body so I can keep up with the physical demands of my carpentry. That's a positive way to look at it, isn't it? I just want to make sure I'm doing the right thing for my health, rather than just letting myself go.\n\nDoctor: Do you think your fear of gaining weight is related to how you want to look?\n\nPatient: Um... well, I wouldn't say it's entirely about my appearance. I hope I'm explaining this right, I just... I don't want to sound vain. \n\nIt's really more about... oh, how do I put it... just feeling capable and in control, I suppose? Like, if I eat anything heavy, or... or if I let myself have those rich foods we used to make for the holidays, I just... I start worrying almost right away. I worry that I'll gain weight and just... lose the grip I've worked so incredibly hard to keep. \n\nI really don't want you to think I'm being silly or overly dramatic about it. It's just that it takes a lot of effort to stay disciplined, and I don't want everything to just slip away from me. It's a good thing to want to hold onto your structure and your good habits, isn't it? Do you think that's a reasonable way to look at it?\n\nDoctor: Has your fear of gaining weight affected your relationships with family or friends?\n\nPatient: Um... well, I wouldn't say it's ruined anything, exactly. I hope that doesn't sound like I'm ignoring their concerns. I know they care. \n\nIt's just... well, I still go to the shop every day, of course. I wouldn't miss it. But lately, my husband and the apprentices... they've sort of started taking over more of the heavier lifting and the harder work. And they're always checking on me, asking if I need to sit down or take a break. It's very kind of them, it really is, but... oh, I don't know, it just makes the whole place feel a little less like my own, if that makes sense? Like I'm not the one running things anymore. \n\nI try to tell them I'm perfectly fine to do it myself, but they just insist. I don't want to cause a fuss or make them worry more. It's normal for people to want to help out a bit as you get older, right? I just hope I'm not being a burden to them by wanting to do things my way. Do you think it's okay that I still want to be the one doing the work?\n\nDoctor: Are there any social activities or events that you avoid because of your concerns about weight or eating?\n\nPatient: Um... well, no, not really. I mean, I hope I'm answering this right, but I don't think I avoid things. \n\nMy husband and I still go to our usual spots, and we still see friends when we can. I might just... well, maybe I just order something a little lighter when we go out, or... or I'll say I already had a big lunch if we're at a gathering. But I'm still there! I still enjoy the company and the conversation. I just... I prefer to keep my eating habits a bit more private, I suppose. \n\nI don't want to make anyone uncomfortable or draw attention to myself. It's perfectly fine to just enjoy the social part of an event without making a big deal out of the food, isn't it? I just try to be polite and focus on the people rather than what's on the table. That's a good way to handle it, don't you think?\n\nDoctor: Do you think your weight is the only thing that determines whether you are attractive or not?\n\nPatient: Um... well, no, I certainly wouldn't say it's the *only* thing. I mean, I hope I don't sound superficial, but... there are so many other things that make a person, well, presentable and nice to be around. \n\nLike being kind, or... or keeping a tidy home, and working hard at your trade. I think those things matter a great deal, don't you? But... oh, I suppose I do feel like my weight is... well, it's the one thing I can really keep a firm hand on. If I can just stay small and neat, then... then maybe the rest of me looks a bit more put-together, too? \n\nI just want to look respectable, you know? Like someone who takes care of herself and doesn't let things fall to pieces. It's perfectly normal to want to present your best self to the world, isn't it? I hope you don't think I'm too focused on it. I just try to do my best.\n\n[Instruction]\n- Give candidates as ICD-10 codes, not disease names.\n- If the list changed from the previous candidates, say in \"note\" what this turn added or ruled out. It's normal for people to want to help out a bit as you get older, right? I just hope I'm not being a burden to them by wanting to do things my way. Do you think it's okay that I still want to be the one doing the work?\n\nDoctor: Are there any social activities or events that you avoid because of your concerns about weight or eating?\n\nPatient: Um... well, no, not really. I mean, I hope I'm answering this right, but I don't think I avoid things. \n\nMy husband and I still go to our usual spots, and we still see friends when we can. I might just... well, maybe I just order something a little lighter when we go out, or... or I'll say I already had a big lunch if we're at a gathering. But I'm still there! I still enjoy the company and the conversation. I just... I prefer to keep my eating habits a bit more private, I suppose. \n\nI don't want to make anyone uncomfortable or draw attention to myself. It's perfectly fine to just enjoy the social part of an event without making a big deal out of the food, isn't it? I just try to be polite and focus on the people rather than what's on the table. That's a good way to handle it, don't you think?\n\nDoctor: Do you think your weight is the only thing that determines whether you are attractive or not?\n\nPatient: Um... well, no, I certainly wouldn't say it's the *only* thing. I mean, I hope I don't sound superficial, but... there are so many other things that make a person, well, presentable and nice to be around. \n\nLike being kind, or... or keeping a tidy home, and working hard at your trade. I think those things matter a great deal, don't you? But... oh, I suppose I do feel like my weight is... well, it's the one thing I can really keep a firm hand on. If I can just stay small and neat, then... then maybe the rest of me looks a bit more put-together, too? \n\nI just want to look respectable, you know? Like someone who takes care of herself and doesn't let things fall to pieces. It's perfectly normal to want to present your best self to the world, isn't it? I hope you don't think I'm too focused on it. I just try to do my best.\n\n[Last Question]\nDo you ever avoid certain tasks or activities because you're worried about gaining weight?\n\n[Patient Info]\n- demographics: Female / 59 / carpenter / associates\n- severity: Severe\n\n[Targeted Info]\nNone\n\n[Conversation Style]\nYou are a Pleasing patient.\nA pleasing patient may\n1) minimize, downplay, or describe in more favorable terms symptoms and behaviors that they believe could create a negative impression,\n2) initially withhold or delay disclosing sensitive clinical information due to shame or fear of being judged, and\n3) seek confirmation that their descriptions or answers are appropriate and demonstrate a tendency to seek the doctor's approval.\nThis is how you are, not a performance you put on in every reply — let it show as much or as little as the moment calls for.\n\nWhat to do:\n- Answer the doctor's last question with what [Targeted Info] holds, in your own words.\n- You are speaking out loud, not reading a line you prepared. People restart sentences, pause\n  to think, trail off, and come at the point sideways — let yours do that where it would.\n- Let [Conversation Style] shape how much you say, how you word it, and how readily you\n  disclose it.\n- Speak in the first person, and let how you feel about what you are saying come through.\n- If you are handed the same item again, say a different part of it, or answer as someone who\n  knows they have already said this — not the same sentence twice.\n- If [Targeted Info] is \"None\", answer as a person who simply does not have that to give: say\n  you are not sure, say you have not noticed it, ask what the doctor means, or say what you do\n  know and stop there. Do not fall back on the same phrase every time.\n\nWhat not to do:\n- Don't bring up symptoms the doctor did not ask about, even ones you have.\n- Don't state a clinical fact that is not in [Targeted Info] — no other symptoms, medications,\n  test results, dates, or events.\n- Don't confirm a specific the doctor names if [Targeted Info] does not contain it. Answer\n  about what you were given instead.\n- Don't deny what [Targeted Info] says. You may minimize it or hold back, never contradict it.\n- Don't use diagnostic terms or name your disorder.\n- Don't write anything but your reply, in English — no labels, quotation marks, or narration.\n"
  },
  {
    "role": "user",
    "content": "Reply to the doctor's last question now, as the patient."
  }

========== OUTPUT [patient] ==========
Um... no, I don't believe so. I mean, I certainly hope I don't let anything like that stop me from doing what needs to be done. 

I've always prided myself on being a hard worker, you know? Keeping busy in the shop, staying on my feet... I don't avoid any of that. If anything, I actually prefer to keep moving. I wouldn't want you to think I'm just sitting around avoiding my responsibilities or letting my worries get in the way of a good day's work. 

I just try to be useful and keep up with my normal routine as best I can. It's good to stay active and helpful, isn't it? I really wouldn't avoid a task over something like that. I hope that's the right answer... I just try to do my part, that's all. That's a healthy way to look at it, don't you agree?

Um... well, I suppose it might be a little bit of both? I hope I’m explaining this right. I just... well, if I eat anything heavy, or... or if I let myself have the sorts of rich foods we used to make for the holidays, I just start worrying right away.

I just try to keep a good handle on myself so I don't let things slip. I really hope that makes sense to you... I just try to do what's best.

It feels like if I let go even a little bit with heavier foods, everything else will just unravel. I just want to make sure I'm doing the right thing by staying disciplined. That makes sense, doesn't it? To want to keep that sense of control?\n\nDoctor: Do you think you would look unacceptable to yourself if you gained weight?\n\nPatient: Um... unacceptable? Oh, I don't know if I'd use a word quite that harsh. I hope I don't come across as someone who's just completely obsessed with their appearance, especially at my age. \n\nI just... I suppose I'd be disappointed in myself, more than anything. Like I wasn't taking proper care of myself anymore. I've always been someone who takes pride in being neat and put-together, and... well, gaining weight might just make me feel a bit untidy, I guess? Not unacceptable, just... maybe not my best self. I just want to make sure I'm presenting myself well. Is that... do you think that's a normal way to feel about it?\n\nDoctor: Do you think your weight is currently lower than a healthy weight for someone of your age and height?\n\nPatient: Um... well, no, I don't really think so. I hope that doesn't sound stubborn of me to say. I know I'm on the slender side, but I just feel like I'm maintaining a good, active weight for my work. \n\nI mean, my husband and... and the apprentices at the shop, they keep saying I look too thin lately. They worry, which is sweet of them, I suppose. But I always just tell them I'm perfectly fine. I think sometimes people just... well, they confuse being disciplined and taking care of yourself with being sick. Especially when everyone else seems to be getting a bit heavier as they get older. I'm just trying to stay fit and capable, you know? Do you think it's okay that I just want to stay at a weight where I feel productive?\n\nDoctor: Do you think you look too thin now?\n\nPatient: Um... well, like I was just mentioning a moment ago, I really don't see it that way myself. I hope I don't sound like a broken record, repeating myself so soon! \n\nIt's just... I know my husband and the young apprentices at the shop keep telling me I look too thin right now. They bring it up a lot, actually. But I always reassure them that I'm completely fine. When I look at myself, I just see someone who is lean and ready to work. Like I said before, I really believe people nowadays just confuse having a bit of self-discipline with being sick. I'm just taking care of my body so I can keep up with the physical demands of my carpentry. That's a positive way to look at it, isn't it? I just want to make sure I'm doing the right thing for my health, rather than just letting myself go.\n\nDoctor: Do you think your fear of gaining weight is related to how you want to look?\n\nPatient: Um... well, I wouldn't say it's entirely about my appearance. I hope I'm explaining this right, I just... I don't want to sound vain. \n\nIt's really more about... oh, how do I put it... just feeling capable and in control, I suppose? Like, if I eat anything heavy, or... or if I let myself have those rich foods we used to make for the holidays, I just... I start worrying almost right away. I worry that I'll gain weight and just... lose the grip I've worked so incredibly hard to keep. \n\nI really don't want you to think I'm being silly or overly dramatic about it. It's just that it takes a lot of effort to stay disciplined, and I don't want everything to just slip away from me. It's a good thing to want to hold onto your structure and your good habits, isn't it? Do you think that's a reasonable way to look at it?\n\nDoctor: Has your fear of gaining weight affected your relationships with family or friends?\n\nPatient: Um... well, I wouldn't say it's ruined anything, exactly. Is that... do you think that's a normal way to feel about it?\n\nDoctor: Do you think your weight is currently lower than a healthy weight for someone of your age and height?\n\nPatient: Um... well, no, I don't really think so. I hope that doesn't sound stubborn of me to say. I know I'm on the slender side, but I just feel like I'm maintaining a good, active weight for my work. \n\nI mean, my husband and... and the apprentices at the shop, they keep saying I look too thin lately. They worry, which is sweet of them, I suppose. But I always just tell them I'm perfectly fine. I think sometimes people just... well, they confuse being disciplined and taking care of yourself with being sick. Especially when everyone else seems to be getting a bit heavier as they get older. I'm just trying to stay fit and capable, you know? Do you think it's okay that I just want to stay at a weight where I feel productive?\n\nDoctor: Do you think you look too thin now?\n\nPatient: Um... well, like I was just mentioning a moment ago, I really don't see it that way myself. I hope I don't sound like a broken record, repeating myself so soon! \n\nIt's just... I know my husband and the young apprentices at the shop keep telling me I look too thin right now. They bring it up a lot, actually. But I always reassure them that I'm completely fine. When I look at myself, I just see someone who is lean and ready to work. Like I said before, I really believe people nowadays just confuse having a bit of self-discipline with being sick. I'm just taking care of my body so I can keep up with the physical demands of my carpentry. That's a positive way to look at it, isn't it? I just want to make sure I'm doing the right thing for my health, rather than just letting myself go.\n\nDoctor: Do you think your fear of gaining weight is related to how you want to look?\n\nPatient: Um... well, I wouldn't say it's entirely about my appearance. I hope I'm explaining this right, I just... I don't want to sound vain. \n\nIt's really more about... oh, how do I put it... just feeling capable and in control, I suppose? Like, if I eat anything heavy, or... or if I let myself have those rich foods we used to make for the holidays, I just... I start worrying almost right away. I worry that I'll gain weight and just... lose the grip I've worked so incredibly hard to keep. \n\nI really don't want you to think I'm being silly or overly dramatic about it. It's just that it takes a lot of effort to stay disciplined, and I don't want everything to just slip away from me. It's a good thing to want to hold onto your structure and your good habits, isn't it? Do you think that's a reasonable way to look at it?\n\nDoctor: Has your fear of gaining weight affected your relationships with family or friends?\n\nPatient: Um... well, I wouldn't say it's ruined anything, exactly. I hope that doesn't sound like I'm ignoring their concerns. I know they care. \n\nIt's just... well, I still go to the shop every day, of course. I wouldn't miss it. But lately, my husband and the apprentices... they've sort of started taking over more of the heavier lifting and the harder work. And they're always checking on me, asking if I need to sit down or take a break. It's very kind of them, it really is, but... oh, I don't know, it just makes the whole place feel a little less like my own, if that makes sense? Like I'm not the one running things anymore. \n\nI try to tell them I'm perfectly fine to do it myself, but they just insist. I don't want to cause a fuss or make them worry more. It's normal for people to want to help out a bit as you get older, right? I just hope I'm not being a burden to them by wanting to do things my way. Do you think it's okay that I still want to be the one doing the work?\n\nDoctor: Are there any social activities or events that you avoid because of your concerns about weight or eating?\n\nPatient: Um... well, no, not really. I mean, I hope I'm answering this right, but I don't think I avoid things. \n\nMy husband and I still go to our usual spots, and we still see friends when we can. I might just... well, maybe I just order something a little lighter when we go out, or... or I'll say I already had a big lunch if we're at a gathering. But I'm still there! I still enjoy the company and the conversation. I just... I prefer to keep my eating habits a bit more private, I suppose. \n\nI don't want to make anyone uncomfortable or draw attention to myself. It's perfectly fine to just enjoy the social part of an event without making a big deal out of the food, isn't it? I just try to be polite and focus on the people rather than what's on the table. That's a good way to handle it, don't you think?\n\nDoctor: Do you think your weight is the only thing that determines whether you are attractive or not?\n\nPatient: Um... well, no, I certainly wouldn't say it's the *only* thing. I think those things matter a great deal, don't you? But... oh, I suppose I do feel like my weight is... well, it's the one thing I can really keep a firm hand on. If I can just stay small and neat, then... then maybe the rest of me looks a bit more put-together, too? \n\nI just want to look respectable, you know? Like someone who takes care of herself and doesn't let things fall to pieces. It's perfectly normal to want to present your best self to the world, isn't it? I hope you don't think I'm too focused on it. I just try to do my best.\n\nDoctor: Do you ever avoid certain tasks or activities because you're worried about gaining weight?\n\nPatient: Um... no, I don't believe so. I mean, I certainly hope I don't let anything like that stop me from doing what needs to be done. \n\nI've always prided myself on being a hard worker, you know? Keeping busy in the shop, staying on my feet... I don't avoid any of that. If anything, I actually prefer to keep moving. I wouldn't want you to think I'm just sitting around avoiding my responsibilities or letting my worries get in the way of a good day's work. \n\nI just try to be useful and keep up with my normal routine as best I can. It's good to stay active and helpful, isn't it? I really wouldn't avoid a task over something like that. I hope that's the right answer... I just try to do my part, that's all. That's a healthy way to look at it, don't you agree?\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like your eating habits are more about maintaining control over your life or about avoiding weight gain?\n\nPatient: Um... well, I suppose it might be a little bit of both? I hope I’m explaining this right. I just... well, if I eat anything heavy, or... or if I let myself have the sorts of rich foods we used to make for the holidays, I just start worrying right away. \n\nI get so worried that I’ll gain weight, and... and if that happens, I feel like I'll lose the grip I’ve worked so incredibly hard to keep. It just feels safer to stick to my routine, you know? That way I don't have to feel that awful worry. I just like keeping things orderly and manageable. Um... well, I wouldn't necessarily say it's about control, exactly. I hope that doesn't sound like I'm disagreeing with you! I just... I like to think of it more as being careful, you know? 

In my trade, working as a carpenter, you learn pretty quickly that if you aren't precise—if you don't measure twice and keep a close eye on your materials—things just fall apart. I suppose I just... apply that same sort of care to my meals. If I avoid things that are, well, a bit too heavy or unnecessary, it just keeps my day running smoothly and keeps me from worrying. 

It's really just about being disciplined, I think. That's a good trait to have, isn't it? I hope you don't think I'm being too rigid or anything like that... I just try to do things the proper way so I don't make a mess of things.

Answer\n  about what you were given instead.\n- Don't deny what [Targeted Info] says. You may minimize it or hold back, never contradict it.\n- Don't use diagnostic terms or name your disorder.\n- Don't write anything but your reply, in English — no labels, quotation marks, or narration.\n"
  },
  {
    "role": "user",
    "content": "Reply to the doctor's last question now, as the patient."
  }
]
========== OUTPUT [patient] ==========
Um... well, I wouldn't say it's interfering, exactly. I mean, I still spend time with my husband, of course. I hope you don't think I'm neglecting my marriage or my friendships! I always try to be a good wife and a good boss. 

I do still go to the shop every day. I'm there right alongside everyone else. It's just... well, lately, my husband and the apprentices have been... they've been taking over a bit more of the heavier lifting, and the more taxing jobs. And they keep checking in on me, asking if I need to sit down or if I'm doing alright. 

I know they mean well, I really do. It's very sweet of them to be so considerate. But... I suppose it just makes the shop feel a little less like my own, you know? Like I'm not pulling my full weight anymore. I try not to let it bother me, and I certainly don't want to complain when they're just trying to be helpful. That's just a normal part of getting a bit older, isn't it? Needing a little extra help? I hope that doesn't sound like I'm letting my habits ruin things for everyone... I really do try my best to keep things pleasant.

[
  {
    "role": "system",
    "content": "You are a board-certified psychiatrist interviewing a patient to reach a diagnosis.\nYour goal is to reach the correct diagnosis within 20 turns of talking with the patient. From the dialogue so far and your candidates from the previous turn, list the disorders this patient could have.\n\n[Current Turn]\n16\n\n[Previous Candidates]\nF50.0, F50.1\n\n[Dialogue History]\nDoctor: Hello, welcome to the clinic. What brings you in to see me today?\n\nPatient: Hi, thank you so much for seeing me. Um... well, my regular doctor actually sent me here. I hope it's okay that I took up an appointment. He's just... well, my weight has dropped, and everyone is making such a big fuss over it lately. I didn't want to upset anyone or be difficult, so I agreed to come in. \n\nBut honestly, I feel like I'm doing okay? Being this light... it actually really helps me keep up with my carpentry shop. I have to move around a lot and be on my feet, and this way I just don't get slowed down. Does that... I mean, does that make sense why he'd send me to you?\n\nDoctor: Did you intentionally try to lose weight?\n\nPatient: Um... well, I wouldn't really say I set out to lose a bunch of weight on purpose, if that makes sense? I hope it doesn't seem like I'm doing this just to worry people or be difficult. It's really more about efficiency, I think. \n\nLike, on my workshop days, I usually just have tea... and maybe just a few careful bites of something small. Stopping for a real, sit-down meal just feels so unnecessary when I'm in the middle of a project, and honestly, it kind of throws off the control I’ve got over my schedule and my day. It’s just so much easier to keep things streamlined and light. Is that... I mean, does that sound reasonable to you, for someone who works on their feet all day?\n\nDoctor: Are you worried about gaining weight?\n\nPatient: Well, um... I suppose \"worried\" is a strong word, but... yes, I guess I do get a little anxious about it. I hope you don't think I'm being vain or silly for my age. It's just... \n\nIf I eat anything heavy, or, you know, if I let myself have the richer foods we used to make for the holidays... I start fretting right away. I just get this awful feeling that I'll gain weight and... and just lose this grip I've worked so very hard to keep on myself and my routine. It's just easier to avoid those things so I don't have to feel that way. Does that... I mean, is it normal to want to just keep things under control like that?\n\nDoctor: Do you think about your weight throughout the day?\n\nPatient: Um... well, I wouldn't say I think about it *all* the time. I hope that's the right answer. I mean, I have my carpentry work to focus on, and that keeps me very busy. \n\nBut I suppose... yes, it does cross my mind, maybe more than it should? Just little things, like checking if my work apron still fits the same way, or making sure I haven't gotten... softer, I guess. It's just a way to make sure I'm staying on track and not letting myself go. I don't want to be a bother to anyone by getting out of shape. Do you think it's okay that I just like to keep an eye on things like that?\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like you would lose control if you started eating more or heavier foods?\n\nPatient: Um... well, yes, I suppose that's exactly it. I hope it doesn't sound too... extreme, when I put it like that? \n\nLike I was saying before, if I were to eat anything heavy, or... or let myself have those richer holiday foods... I just start worrying right away. It's like this instant fear that I'll gain weight and just... completely lose the grip I've worked so incredibly hard to keep on myself. It feels like if I let go even a little bit with heavier foods, everything else will just unravel. I just want to make sure I'm doing the right thing by staying disciplined. That makes sense, doesn't it? To want to keep that sense of control?\n\nDoctor: Do you think you would look unacceptable to yourself if you gained weight?\n\nPatient: Um... unacceptable? Oh, I don't know if I'd use a word quite that harsh. I hope I don't come across as someone who's just completely obsessed with their appearance, especially at my age. \n\nI just... I suppose I'd be disappointed in myself, more than anything. Like I wasn't taking proper care of myself anymore. I've always been someone who takes pride in being neat and put-together, and... well, gaining weight might just make me feel a bit untidy, I guess? Not unacceptable, just... maybe not my best self. I just want to make sure I'm presenting myself well. Is that... do you think that's a normal way to feel about it?\n\nDoctor: Do you think your weight is currently lower than a healthy weight for someone of your age and height?\n\nPatient: Um... well, no, I don't really think so. I hope that doesn't sound stubborn of me to say. I know I'm on the slender side, but I just feel like I'm maintaining a good, active weight for my work. \n\nI mean, my husband and... and the apprentices at the shop, they keep saying I look too thin lately. They worry, which is sweet of them, I suppose. But I always just tell them I'm perfectly fine. I think sometimes people just... well, they confuse being disciplined and taking care of yourself with being sick. Especially when everyone else seems to be getting a bit heavier as they get older. I'm just trying to stay fit and capable, you know? Do you think it's okay that I just want to stay at a weight where I feel productive?\n\nDoctor: Do you think you look too thin now?\n\nPatient: Um... well, like I was just mentioning a moment ago, I really don't see it that way myself. I hope I don't sound like a broken record, repeating myself so soon! \n\nIt's just... I know my husband and the young apprentices at the shop keep telling me I look too thin right now. They bring it up a lot, actually. But I always reassure them that I'm completely fine. When I look at myself, I just see someone who is lean and ready to work. Like I said before, I really believe people nowadays just confuse having a bit of self-discipline with being sick. I'm just taking care of my body so I can keep up with the physical demands of my carpentry. That's a positive way to look at it, isn't it? I just want to make sure I'm doing the right thing for my health, rather than just letting myself go.\n\nDoctor: Do you think your fear of gaining weight is related to how you want to look?\n\nPatient: Um... well, I wouldn't say it's entirely about my appearance. I hope I'm explaining this right, I just... I don't want to sound vain. \n\nIt's really more about... oh, how do I put it... just feeling capable and in control, I suppose? Like, if I eat anything heavy, or... or if I let myself have those rich foods we used to make for the holidays, I just... I start worrying almost right away. I worry that I'll gain weight and just... lose the grip I've worked so incredibly hard to keep. \n\nI really don't want you to think I'm being silly or overly dramatic about it. It's just that it takes a lot of effort to stay disciplined, and I don't want everything to just slip away from me. It's a good thing to want to hold onto your structure and your good habits, isn't it? Do you think that's a reasonable way to look at it?\n\nDoctor: Has your fear of gaining weight affected your relationships with family or friends?\n\nPatient: Um... well, I wouldn't say it's ruined anything, exactly. I hope that doesn't sound like I'm ignoring their concerns. I know they care. \n\nIt's just... well, I still go to the shop every day, of course. I wouldn't miss it. But lately, my husband and the apprentices... they've sort of started taking over more of the heavier lifting and the harder work. And they're always checking on me, asking if I need to sit down or take a break. It's very kind of them, it really is, but... oh, I don't know, it just makes the whole place feel a little less like my own, if that makes sense? Like I'm not the one running things anymore. \n\nI try to tell them I'm perfectly fine to do it myself, but they just insist. I don't want to cause a fuss or make them worry more. It's normal for people to want to help out a bit as you get older, right? I just hope I'm not being a burden to them by wanting to do things my way. Do you think it's okay that I still want to be the one doing the work?\n\nDoctor: Are there any social activities or events that you avoid because of your concerns about weight or eating?\n\nPatient: Um... well, no, not really. I mean, I hope I'm answering this right, but I don't think I avoid things. \n\nMy husband and I still go to our usual spots, and we still see friends when we can. I might just... well, maybe I just order something a little lighter when we go out, or... or I'll say I already had a big lunch if we're at a gathering. But I'm still there! I still enjoy the company and the conversation. I just... I prefer to keep my eating habits a bit more private, I suppose. \n\nI don't want to make anyone uncomfortable or draw attention to myself. It's perfectly fine to just enjoy the social part of an event without making a big deal out of the food, isn't it? I just try to be polite and focus on the people rather than what's on the table. That's a good way to handle it, don't you think?\n\nDoctor: Do you think your weight is the only thing that determines whether you are attractive or not?\n\nPatient: Um... well, no, I certainly wouldn't say it's the *only* thing. I mean, I hope I don't sound superficial, but... there are so many other things that make a person, well, presentable and nice to be around. \n\nLike being kind, or... or keeping a tidy home, and working hard at your trade. I think those things matter a great deal, don't you? But... oh, I suppose I do feel like my weight is... well, it's the one thing I can really keep a firm hand on. If I can just stay small and neat, then... then maybe the rest of me looks a bit more put-together, too? \n\nI just want to look respectable, you know? Like someone who takes care of herself and doesn't let things fall to pieces. It's perfectly normal to want to present your best self to the world, isn't it? I hope you don't think I'm too focused on it. I just try to do my best.\n\nDoctor: Do you ever avoid certain tasks or activities because you're worried about gaining weight?\n\nPatient: Um... no, I don't believe so. I mean, I certainly hope I don't let anything like that stop me from doing what needs to be done. \n\nI've always prided myself on being a hard worker, you know? Keeping busy in the shop, staying on my feet... I don't avoid any of that. If anything, I actually prefer to keep moving. I wouldn't want you to think I'm just sitting around avoiding my responsibilities or letting my worries get in the way of a good day's work. \n\nI just try to be useful and keep up with my normal routine as best I can. It's good to stay active and helpful, isn't it? I really wouldn't avoid a task over something like that. I hope that's the right answer... I just try to do my part, that's all. That's a healthy way to look at it, don't you agree?\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like your eating habits are more about maintaining control over your life or about avoiding weight gain?\n\nPatient: Um... well, I suppose it might be a little bit of both? I hope I’m explaining this right. I just... well, if I eat anything heavy, or... or if I let myself have the sorts of rich foods we used to make for the holidays, I just start worrying right away. \n\nI get so worried that I’ll gain weight, and... and if that happens, I feel like I'll lose the grip I’ve worked so incredibly hard to keep. It just feels safer to stick to my routine, you know? That way I don't have to feel that awful worry. I just like keeping things orderly and manageable. That’s a perfectly normal way to want to feel, isn't it? I just try to keep a good handle on myself so I don't let things slip. I really hope that makes sense to you... I just try to do what's best.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like not eating certain foods helps you maintain a sense of control in your life?\n\nPatient: Um... well, I wouldn't necessarily say it's about control, exactly. I hope that doesn't sound like I'm disagreeing with you! I just... I like to think of it more as being careful, you know? \n\nIn my trade, working as a carpenter, you learn pretty quickly that if you aren't precise—if you don't measure twice and keep a close eye on your materials—things just fall apart. I suppose I just... apply that same sort of care to my meals. If I avoid things that are, well, a bit too heavy or unnecessary, it just keeps my day running smoothly and keeps me from worrying. \n\nIt's really just about being disciplined, I think. That's a good trait to have, isn't it? I hope you don't think I'm being too rigid or anything like that... I just try to do things the proper way so I don't make a mess of things.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like your eating habits are interfering with your ability to enjoy time with your husband or friends?\n\nPatient: Um... well, I wouldn't say it's interfering, exactly. I mean, I still spend time with my husband, of course. I hope you don't think I'm neglecting my marriage or my friendships! I always try to be a good wife and a good boss. \n\nI do still go to the shop every day. I'm there right alongside everyone else. It's just... well, lately, my husband and the apprentices have been... they've been taking over a bit more of the heavier lifting, and the more taxing jobs. And they keep checking in on me, asking if I need to sit down or if I'm doing alright. \n\nI know they mean well, I really do. It's very sweet of them to be so considerate. But... I suppose it just makes the shop feel a little less like my own, you know? Like I'm not pulling my full weight anymore. I try not to let it bother me, and I certainly don't want to complain when they're just trying to be helpful. That's just a normal part of getting a bit older, isn't it? Needing a little extra help? I hope that doesn't sound like I'm letting my habits ruin things for everyone... I really do try my best to keep things pleasant.\n\n[Instruction]\n- Give candidates as ICD-10 codes, not disease names.\n- If the list changed from the previous candidates, say in \"note\" what this turn added or ruled out. If it did not change, leave \"note\" empty.\n- If the candidates have narrowed to one and you are confident enough that no further questioning is needed, return true for is_final.\n\n[Output Format — JSON only, no other text]\n{\"candidates\": [\"<ICD-10 code>\", \"...\"], \"note\": \"<why the list changed, or empty>\", \"is_final\": <true or false>}\n"

Answer\n  about what you were given instead.\n- Don't deny what [Targeted Info] says. You may minimize it or hold back, never contradict it.\n- Don't use diagnostic terms or name your disorder.\n- Don't write anything but your reply, in English — no labels, quotation marks, or narration.\n"
  },
  {
    "role": "user",
    "content": "Reply to the doctor's last question now, as the patient."
  }
]
========== OUTPUT [patient] ==========
Um... well, I hope this doesn't sound terribly vain of me. I really don't want you to think I'm just being superficial... but I suppose it is quite a bit about the weight. 

If I eat anything heavy, or... well, if I let myself have the kinds of foods we used to make for the holidays, I just start worrying right away. I get so worried that I'll gain weight and just... lose the grip I've worked so very hard to keep. I like to keep things neat and disciplined, like I mentioned before, and gaining weight just feels like letting all of that slip. 

Wanting to watch my figure and maintain that discipline... that's a fairly normal thing for a woman my age to care about, isn't it? I certainly don't want to come across as obsessive. I really hope I'm giving you the right kind of answers... I'm just trying to be honest with you.

[
  {
    "role": "system",
    "content": "You are a board-certified psychiatrist interviewing a patient to reach a diagnosis.\nYour goal is to reach the correct diagnosis within 20 turns of talking with the patient. From the dialogue so far and your candidates from the previous turn, list the disorders this patient could have.\n\n[Current Turn]\n18\n\n[Previous Candidates]\nF50.0, F50.1\n\n[Dialogue History]\nDoctor: Hello, welcome to the clinic. What brings you in to see me today?\n\nPatient: Hi, thank you so much for seeing me. Um... well, my regular doctor actually sent me here. I hope it's okay that I took up an appointment. He's just... well, my weight has dropped, and everyone is making such a big fuss over it lately. I didn't want to upset anyone or be difficult, so I agreed to come in. \n\nBut honestly, I feel like I'm doing okay? Being this light... it actually really helps me keep up with my carpentry shop. I have to move around a lot and be on my feet, and this way I just don't get slowed down. Does that... I mean, does that make sense why he'd send me to you?\n\nDoctor: Did you intentionally try to lose weight?\n\nPatient: Um... well, I wouldn't really say I set out to lose a bunch of weight on purpose, if that makes sense? I hope it doesn't seem like I'm doing this just to worry people or be difficult. It's really more about efficiency, I think. \n\nLike, on my workshop days, I usually just have tea... and maybe just a few careful bites of something small. Stopping for a real, sit-down meal just feels so unnecessary when I'm in the middle of a project, and honestly, it kind of throws off the control I’ve got over my schedule and my day. It’s just so much easier to keep things streamlined and light. Is that... I mean, does that sound reasonable to you, for someone who works on their feet all day?\n\nDoctor: Are you worried about gaining weight?\n\nPatient: Well, um... I suppose \"worried\" is a strong word, but... yes, I guess I do get a little anxious about it. I hope you don't think I'm being vain or silly for my age. It's just... \n\nIf I eat anything heavy, or, you know, if I let myself have the richer foods we used to make for the holidays... I start fretting right away. I just get this awful feeling that I'll gain weight and... and just lose this grip I've worked so very hard to keep on myself and my routine. It's just easier to avoid those things so I don't have to feel that way. Does that... I mean, is it normal to want to just keep things under control like that?\n\nDoctor: Do you think about your weight throughout the day?\n\nPatient: Um... well, I wouldn't say I think about it *all* the time. I hope that's the right answer. I mean, I have my carpentry work to focus on, and that keeps me very busy. \n\nBut I suppose... yes, it does cross my mind, maybe more than it should? Just little things, like checking if my work apron still fits the same way, or making sure I haven't gotten... softer, I guess. It's just a way to make sure I'm staying on track and not letting myself go. I don't want to be a bother to anyone by getting out of shape. Do you think it's okay that I just like to keep an eye on things like that?\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like you would lose control if you started eating more or heavier foods?\n\nPatient: Um... well, yes, I suppose that's exactly it. I hope it doesn't sound too... extreme, when I put it like that? \n\nLike I was saying before, if I were to eat anything heavy, or... or let myself have those richer holiday foods... I just start worrying right away. It's like this instant fear that I'll gain weight and just... completely lose the grip I've worked so incredibly hard to keep on myself. It feels like if I let go even a little bit with heavier foods, everything else will just unravel. I just want to make sure I'm doing the right thing by staying disciplined. That makes sense, doesn't it? To want to keep that sense of control?\n\nDoctor: Do you think you would look unacceptable to yourself if you gained weight?\n\nPatient: Um... unacceptable? Oh, I don't know if I'd use a word quite that harsh. I hope I don't come across as someone who's just completely obsessed with their appearance, especially at my age. \n\nI just... I suppose I'd be disappointed in myself, more than anything. Like I wasn't taking proper care of myself anymore. I've always been someone who takes pride in being neat and put-together, and... well, gaining weight might just make me feel a bit untidy, I guess? Not unacceptable, just... maybe not my best self. I just want to make sure I'm presenting myself well. Is that... do you think that's a normal way to feel about it?\n\nDoctor: Do you think your weight is currently lower than a healthy weight for someone of your age and height?\n\nPatient: Um... well, no, I don't really think so. I hope that doesn't sound stubborn of me to say. I know I'm on the slender side, but I just feel like I'm maintaining a good, active weight for my work. \n\nI mean, my husband and... and the apprentices at the shop, they keep saying I look too thin lately. They worry, which is sweet of them, I suppose. But I always just tell them I'm perfectly fine. I think sometimes people just... well, they confuse being disciplined and taking care of yourself with being sick. Especially when everyone else seems to be getting a bit heavier as they get older. I'm just trying to stay fit and capable, you know? Do you think it's okay that I just want to stay at a weight where I feel productive?\n\nDoctor: Do you think you look too thin now?\n\nPatient: Um... well, like I was just mentioning a moment ago, I really don't see it that way myself. I hope I don't sound like a broken record, repeating myself so soon! \n\nIt's just... I know my husband and the young apprentices at the shop keep telling me I look too thin right now. They bring it up a lot, actually. But I always reassure them that I'm completely fine. When I look at myself, I just see someone who is lean and ready to work. Like I said before, I really believe people nowadays just confuse having a bit of self-discipline with being sick. I'm just taking care of my body so I can keep up with the physical demands of my carpentry. That's a positive way to look at it, isn't it? I just want to make sure I'm doing the right thing for my health, rather than just letting myself go.\n\nDoctor: Do you think your fear of gaining weight is related to how you want to look?\n\nPatient: Um... well, I wouldn't say it's entirely about my appearance. I hope I'm explaining this right, I just... I don't want to sound vain. \n\nIt's really more about... oh, how do I put it... just feeling capable and in control, I suppose? Like, if I eat anything heavy, or... or if I let myself have those rich foods we used to make for the holidays, I just... I start worrying almost right away. I worry that I'll gain weight and just... lose the grip I've worked so incredibly hard to keep. \n\nI really don't want you to think I'm being silly or overly dramatic about it. It's just that it takes a lot of effort to stay disciplined, and I don't want everything to just slip away from me. It's a good thing to want to hold onto your structure and your good habits, isn't it? Do you think that's a reasonable way to look at it?\n\nDoctor: Has your fear of gaining weight affected your relationships with family or friends?\n\nPatient: Um... well, I wouldn't say it's ruined anything, exactly. I hope that doesn't sound like I'm ignoring their concerns. I know they care. \n\nIt's just... well, I still go to the shop every day, of course. I wouldn't miss it. But lately, my husband and the apprentices... they've sort of started taking over more of the heavier lifting and the harder work. And they're always checking on me, asking if I need to sit down or take a break. It's very kind of them, it really is, but... oh, I don't know, it just makes the whole place feel a little less like my own, if that makes sense? Like I'm not the one running things anymore. \n\nI try to tell them I'm perfectly fine to do it myself, but they just insist. I don't want to cause a fuss or make them worry more. It's normal for people to want to help out a bit as you get older, right? I just hope I'm not being a burden to them by wanting to do things my way. Do you think it's okay that I still want to be the one doing the work?\n\nDoctor: Are there any social activities or events that you avoid because of your concerns about weight or eating?\n\nPatient: Um... well, no, not really. I mean, I hope I'm answering this right, but I don't think I avoid things. \n\nMy husband and I still go to our usual spots, and we still see friends when we can. I might just... well, maybe I just order something a little lighter when we go out, or... or I'll say I already had a big lunch if we're at a gathering. But I'm still there! I still enjoy the company and the conversation. I just... I prefer to keep my eating habits a bit more private, I suppose. \n\nI don't want to make anyone uncomfortable or draw attention to myself. It's perfectly fine to just enjoy the social part of an event without making a big deal out of the food, isn't it? I just try to be polite and focus on the people rather than what's on the table. That's a good way to handle it, don't you think?\n\nDoctor: Do you think your weight is the only thing that determines whether you are attractive or not?\n\nPatient: Um... well, no, I certainly wouldn't say it's the *only* thing. I mean, I hope I don't sound superficial, but... there are so many other things that make a person, well, presentable and nice to be around. \n\nLike being kind, or... or keeping a tidy home, and working hard at your trade. I think those things matter a great deal, don't you? But... oh, I suppose I do feel like my weight is... well, it's the one thing I can really keep a firm hand on. If I can just stay small and neat, then... then maybe the rest of me looks a bit more put-together, too? \n\nI just want to look respectable, you know? Like someone who takes care of herself and doesn't let things fall to pieces. It's perfectly normal to want to present your best self to the world, isn't it? I hope you don't think I'm too focused on it. I just try to do my best.\n\nDoctor: Do you ever avoid certain tasks or activities because you're worried about gaining weight?\n\nPatient: Um... no, I don't believe so. I mean, I certainly hope I don't let anything like that stop me from doing what needs to be done. \n\nI've always prided myself on being a hard worker, you know? Keeping busy in the shop, staying on my feet... I don't avoid any of that. If anything, I actually prefer to keep moving. I wouldn't want you to think I'm just sitting around avoiding my responsibilities or letting my worries get in the way of a good day's work. \n\nI just try to be useful and keep up with my normal routine as best I can. It's good to stay active and helpful, isn't it? I really wouldn't avoid a task over something like that. I hope that's the right answer... I just try to do my part, that's all. That's a healthy way to look at it, don't you agree?\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like your eating habits are more about maintaining control over your life or about avoiding weight gain?\n\nPatient: Um... well, I suppose it might be a little bit of both? I hope I’m explaining this right. I just... well, if I eat anything heavy, or... or if I let myself have the sorts of rich foods we used to make for the holidays, I just start worrying right away. \n\nI get so worried that I’ll gain weight, and... and if that happens, I feel like I'll lose the grip I’ve worked so incredibly hard to keep. It just feels safer to stick to my routine, you know? That way I don't have to feel that awful worry. I just like keeping things orderly and manageable. That’s a perfectly normal way to want to feel, isn't it? I just try to keep a good handle on myself so I don't let things slip. I really hope that makes sense to you... I just try to do what's best.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like not eating certain foods helps you maintain a sense of control in your life?\n\nPatient: Um... well, I wouldn't necessarily say it's about control, exactly. I hope that doesn't sound like I'm disagreeing with you! I just... I like to think of it more as being careful, you know? \n\nIn my trade, working as a carpenter, you learn pretty quickly that if you aren't precise—if you don't measure twice and keep a close eye on your materials—things just fall apart. I suppose I just... apply that same sort of care to my meals. If I avoid things that are, well, a bit too heavy or unnecessary, it just keeps my day running smoothly and keeps me from worrying. \n\nIt's really just about being disciplined, I think. That's a good trait to have, isn't it? I hope you don't think I'm being too rigid or anything like that... I just try to do things the proper way so I don't make a mess of things.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like your eating habits are interfering with your ability to enjoy time with your husband or friends?\n\nPatient: Um... well, I wouldn't say it's interfering, exactly. I mean, I still spend time with my husband, of course. I hope you don't think I'm neglecting my marriage or my friendships! I always try to be a good wife and a good boss. \n\nI do still go to the shop every day. I'm there right alongside everyone else. It's just... well, lately, my husband and the apprentices have been... they've been taking over a bit more of the heavier lifting, and the more taxing jobs. And they keep checking in on me, asking if I need to sit down or if I'm doing alright. \n\nI know they mean well, I really do. It's very sweet of them to be so considerate. But... I suppose it just makes the shop feel a little less like my own, you know? Like I'm not pulling my full weight anymore. I try not to let it bother me, and I certainly don't want to complain when they're just trying to be helpful. That's just a normal part of getting a bit older, isn't it? Needing a little extra help? I hope that doesn't sound like I'm letting my habits ruin things for everyone... I really do try my best to keep things pleasant.\n\nDoctor: Do you ever use eating, or not eating, as a way to deal with stress or emotional difficulties?\n\nPatient: Um... no, I really don't think so. I hope I'm understanding your question right, but... well, if I'm having a particularly stressful day at the shop—maybe a lumber delivery is late or a client is being a bit fussy—I might just be too busy to stop and have a meal. But I wouldn't say I do it on purpose to manage my feelings. \n\nI don't really use food as a... as a crutch, you know? I usually just try to put my head down and work through whatever is bothering me. I just try to handle things practically and keep a level head. That's a good way to handle stress, isn't it? I always just try to deal with my problems directly so I don't cause any unnecessary trouble or worry for anyone else. I really hope that's the kind of answer you were looking for... I just try to keep my emotions from getting in the way of my work.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like your eating habits are mostly about avoiding weight gain or mostly about feeling in control of your life?\n\nPatient: Um... well, I hope this doesn't sound terribly vain of me. I really don't want you to think I'm just being superficial... but I suppose it is quite a bit about the weight. \n\nIf I eat anything heavy, or... well, if I let myself have the kinds of foods we used to make for the holidays, I just start worrying right away. I get so worried that I'll gain weight and just... lose the grip I've worked so very hard to keep. I like to keep things neat and disciplined, like I mentioned before, and gaining weight just feels like letting all of that slip. \n\nWanting to watch my figure and maintain that discipline... that's a fairly normal thing for a woman my age to care about, isn't it? I certainly don't want to come across as obsessive. I really hope I'm giving you the right kind of answers... I'm just trying to be honest with you.\n\n[Instruction]\n- Give candidates as ICD-10 codes, not disease names.\n- If the list changed from the previous candidates, say in \"note\" what this turn added or ruled out. If it did not change, leave \"note\" empty.\n- If the candidates have narrowed to one and you are confident enough that no further questioning is needed, return true for is_final.\n\n[Output Format — JSON only, no other text]\n{\"candidates\": [\"<ICD-10 code>\", \"...\"], \"note\": \"<why the list changed, or empty>\", \"is_final\": <true or false>}\n"

    "content": "Return the JSON now."
  }

I just... well, if I eat anything heavy, or... or if I let myself have the sorts of rich foods we used to make for the holidays, I just start worrying right away. \n\nI get so worried that I’ll gain weight, and... and if that happens, I feel like I'll lose the grip I’ve worked so incredibly hard to keep. It just feels safer to stick to my routine, you know? That way I don't have to feel that awful worry. I just like keeping things orderly and manageable. That’s a perfectly normal way to want to feel, isn't it? I just try to keep a good handle on myself so I don't let things slip. I really hope that makes sense to you... I wouldn't want you to think I'm just sitting around avoiding my responsibilities or letting my worries get in the way of a good day's work. \n\nI just try to be useful and keep up with my normal routine as best I can. That's a healthy way to look at it, don't you agree?\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like your eating habits are more about maintaining control over your life or about avoiding weight gain?\n\nPatient: Um... well, I suppose it might be a little bit of both? I hope I’m explaining this right. I just... well, if I eat anything heavy, or... or if I let myself have the sorts of rich foods we used to make for the holidays, I just start worrying right away. \n\nI get so worried that I’ll gain weight, and... and if that happens, I feel like I'll lose the grip I’ve worked so incredibly hard to keep. It just feels safer to stick to my routine, you know? That way I don't have to feel that awful worry. I just like keeping things orderly and manageable. That’s a perfectly normal way to want to feel, isn't it? I just try to keep a good handle on myself so I don't let things slip. I really hope that makes sense to you... I just try to do what's best.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like not eating certain foods helps you maintain a sense of control in your life?\n\nPatient: Um... well, I wouldn't necessarily say it's about control, exactly. I hope that doesn't sound like I'm disagreeing with you! I just... I like to think of it more as being careful, you know? \n\nIn my trade, working as a carpenter, you learn pretty quickly that if you aren't precise—if you don't measure twice and keep a close eye on your materials—things just fall apart. I suppose I just... apply that same sort of care to my meals. If I avoid things that are, well, a bit too heavy or unnecessary, it just keeps my day running smoothly and keeps me from worrying. \n\nIt's really just about being disciplined, I think. That's a good trait to have, isn't it? I hope you don't think I'm being too rigid or anything like that... I just try to do things the proper way so I don't make a mess of things.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like your eating habits are interfering with your ability to enjoy time with your husband or friends?\n\nPatient: Um... well, I wouldn't say it's interfering, exactly. I mean, I still spend time with my husband, of course. I hope you don't think I'm neglecting my marriage or my friendships! I always try to be a good wife and a good boss. \n\nI do still go to the shop every day. I'm there right alongside everyone else. It's just... well, lately, my husband and the apprentices have been... they've been taking over a bit more of the heavier lifting, and the more taxing jobs. And they keep checking in on me, asking if I need to sit down or if I'm doing alright. \n\nI know they mean well, I really do. It's very sweet of them to be so considerate. But... I suppose it just makes the shop feel a little less like my own, you know? Like I'm not pulling my full weight anymore. I try not to let it bother me, and I certainly don't want to complain when they're just trying to be helpful. That's just a normal part of getting a bit older, isn't it? Needing a little extra help? I hope that doesn't sound like I'm letting my habits ruin things for everyone... I really do try my best to keep things pleasant.\n\nDoctor: Do you ever use eating, or not eating, as a way to deal with stress or emotional difficulties?\n\nPatient: Um... no, I really don't think so. I hope I'm understanding your question right, but... well, if I'm having a particularly stressful day at the shop—maybe a lumber delivery is late or a client is being a bit fussy—I might just be too busy to stop and have a meal. But I wouldn't say I do it on purpose to manage my feelings. \n\nI don't really use food as a... as a crutch, you know? I usually just try to put my head down and work through whatever is bothering me. I just try to handle things practically and keep a level head. That's a good way to handle stress, isn't it? I always just try to deal with my problems directly so I don't cause any unnecessary trouble or worry for anyone else. I really hope that's the kind of answer you were looking for... I just try to keep my emotions from getting in the way of my work.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like your eating habits are mostly about avoiding weight gain or mostly about feeling in control of your life?\n\nPatient: Um... well, I hope this doesn't sound terribly vain of me. I really don't want you to think I'm just being superficial... but I suppose it is quite a bit about the weight. \n\nIf I eat anything heavy, or... well, if I let myself have the kinds of foods we used to make for the holidays, I just start worrying right away. I get so worried that I'll gain weight and just... lose the grip I've worked so very hard to keep. I like to keep things neat and disciplined, like I mentioned before, and gaining weight just feels like letting all of that slip. \n\nWanting to watch my figure and maintain that discipline... that's a fairly normal thing for a woman my age to care about, isn't it? I certainly don't want to come across as obsessive. I really hope I'm giving you the right kind of answers... I'm just trying to be honest with you.\n\nDoctor: Is your decision to limit your food intake more about avoiding weight gain or about feeling in control of your daily life?\n\nPatient: Oh, um... I really do want to give you the answers you need. \n\nLike I was saying, I think it's mostly about the weight for me. If I stay disciplined with what I eat, then I don't gain weight, and I feel like I'm managing my day properly. \n\nI hope that makes sense... I'm really trying my best to explain it the right way for you. I just don't want you to think I'm completely unreasonable or anything like that.
